Source431 compact event tape (new C++ UNBUILT/UNRUN; default DOM unchanged):

The reviewed single-inl M7 change packs kind-sized event records while keeping
the complete FDecoder and streaming entry byte-exact. The validating single
parse, shape/unknown/duplicate selection, precise64-bit payloads, one Visit,
Decode helpers, map commit and fallback before destination writes remain.

account; written/replayed bytes shrink, but Reserve still requests Next*16.
No reduced allocated/peak memory or>=1.5x speed claim follows. Benchmark,
tests and the production default DOM path are unchanged.

Source430 finite Editor/Stop8, saved-PIE and Development build/cook evidence (broader gates OPEN):

The final native Stop previously reused one live 256-record scan and could
permanently retain already-quiescent backlogs after the ticker was removed.
It also checked client-slot reclamation before releasing the final counted
input. The reviewed private correction captures the three current input lists
once before callouts, visits each fixed operation store once, deletes only
ready/finished/borrow-free inputs on GT and then rechecks the existing exact
client barriers. Unresolved or genuinely late adoption remains quarantined;
live 256-visit budgets, native authority rules and non-operation barriers stay
unchanged. A shared Control-local GT reclaim depth defers a reentrant Stop
until the active input/store/client/queue mutator has finished. Source001's
missing queue-sweeper boundary was rejected and never adopted; Source002
places ScopeExit before the queue TGuardValue, restoring that guard before
final reclamation on every return. Five new private cases cover one/257 ready
records, a genuinely held tail, direct store/client/input reentry, late stopped
adoption and a real ScopeClosed trace sink reentering the queue sweep.

## Scope ruling (2026-10-20)
Ruling: 2.0 = core M2..M7 + Network Inspector + OpenAPI import + WebSocket + Socket.IO; WebRTC (VaRest-48j)
deferred to 2.x — spec §0.3 says "possibly 2.x", it needs a native WebRTC stack on 5 platforms — cost if
wrong: one more sub-project after release, no rework.
Ruling: Win64/Mac/Android/iOS verification moved from "before M2" to the release pass (VaRest-b4d) — UBT
drops Win64 on a non-Windows host — cost if wrong: MSVC-only warnings found late, fixed in one pass.
Ruling: the four M2 semantics decided in spec §2.2 (a729d8d) — cost if wrong: small API behaviour flips pre-release.
Ruling: push / tag / Fab upload stay owner actions; everything else is done locally on v2, merged
(fast-forward) into develop after each milestone.

## Process per milestone
1. Plan writer subagent (opus) writes docs/superpowers/plans/<date>-varest2-mN-*.md against current code.
2. Plan reviewer subagent checks against spec; controller rules on findings.
3. SDD execution (sdd-workspace per plan), final review, one fix wave.
4. Merge v2 -> develop (ff), checkpoint, bd close milestone.

M5 Source inventory decision (2026-11-03): root-m5-task5-managed-refresh-delivery-source-ruling-v1.json explicitly adds UNBOUNDED transient rejected-refresh callback nodes so the existing void/asynchronous Failed(Config) API can survive an unrestricted refusal burst. Accepted External subscriptions, including ended pending answers, remain capped at64; all other named caps remain. This is an additional exception to the earlier whole-spec/exit inventory ruling, not a fully bounded memory claim. Refusal callbacks remain Control-owned for GT destruction; native wake tasks contain no user callback payload. Source only, with native/compiler/runtime qualification still UNRUN.

## Rulings log (continued)
Ruling (2026-10-20): WebSocket transport = own client on the engine-bundled libwebsockets 3.0 (ThirdParty
libWebSockets, all 5 platforms), not engine IWebSocket — review confirmed silent UTF-8 damage at any frame
boundary (LwsWebSocket.cpp:309-330), cross-kind reordering, garbage close code on empty close frames, Shipping
re-entrancy crash — cost if wrong: one extra W milestone + TLS/cert glue to verify per platform in the release
pass; engine backend can be added later behind IVaRestSocketTransport.
Ruling: server clean 1000 reconnects by default (handler veto / option); protocol-level ping on by default.
Ruling: single plugin; linking claim corrected.
Ruling (2026-10-20): Inspector 2.0 scope cut per review (import/replay/Insights/body search/BP query structs -> 2.x); minimal 13-kind trace contract; UI tests = data layer + construction, widget behaviour manual checklist (Slate does not tick under -nullrhi).
Ruling (2026-10-20): OpenAPI: bulk struct build via exported API + self-check fallback; own strict YAML subset parser; C++ export/remote $ref/adoption/composite auth/OAuth endpoint -> 2.x; Swagger 2.0 droppable stretch.
Ruling (2026-10-20): Socket.IO: websocket-only transport, v5 servers only (2.x refused precisely); interop vs real Node socket.io 4.8.x is a required release gate.

## M2 rulings (from 2026-10-20-varest2-m2-convert-bp/progress.md, workspace deleted after merge 9a3c3a1)
- Ruling: Critical 1 (Json To Struct resets output) + Critical 2 (TryGet special structs refuse null) fixed in plan — reviewer evidence — cost if wrong: none (tests added).
- Ruling: struct-change listener = raw pointer created/deleted in FVaRestEditorModule Startup/Shutdown (two calls in a v1 file body; v1 frozen = paths/signatures) — avoids static-destruction order across .so — cost if wrong: a two-line edit in a v1 file body.
- Ruling: Socket.IO J-1/J-2 writer entries folded into Task 7 (sio-m2-gap.md) — cost if wrong: small extra internal API.
- Ruling: TOptional keeps previous value on mismatch — consistency with the plan's mismatch rule — cost: none.
- Ruling: spec §2.2 wording -> "one warning per repaired string" (plan behaviour kept) — cost if wrong: log volume.

- Ruling (2026-10-21): all 7 Critical findings of both M3 plan reviews accepted as written; C-5 (OnHttpThread + Owner) = pin the owner with TStrongObjectPtr rather than forbid the combination — cost if wrong: owner outlives GC by one callback.

## Continuation rulings (2026-10-23, Codex)
- Ruling: VaRest-81s stays open after W1; it tracks the whole WebSocket subproject W0-W6, and W2 onward remain. W1b outline B8 must close a W1 child task, not this parent epic — verified against its title and the program sequence; cost if wrong: task-state correction only.
- Verified W1b planning finding (2026-10-23, 7c7c3ae): FSocketLink::Push increments PendingInboundBytes only by Event.Bytes.Num(), while reserving slack and enqueuing every Message. Empty messages add0 but occupy queue memory. A payload-only flow threshold cannot bound event metadata for an empty/tiny-message flood and a slow consumer. The W1b planner must resolve this in Review Focus/B4/W3 while preserving the public byte-counter semantics.
- Ruling (2026-10-23): while final W1a reviews run, a read-only W1b design preflight may inspect immutablefbb5007 for proxy callback evidence and metadata/flow-bound decisions (VaRest-81s.8); it performs no source changes/tests/builds and does not start W1b implementation before W1a lands. Why: these known questions precede a complete implementable plan; cost if wrong: recheck recommendations against the final W1a fix delta.

## W1b preflight rulings (2026-10-23, Codex; evidence ws-w1b-design-preflight.md)
- Ruling: after W1a lands, write/review/execute a narrow B1 CONNECT proxy fixture+raw probe prerequisite plan before complete B2-B9 implementation code. Measure ordered APPEND/WSI_CREATE/certificate callbacks and actual CONNECTION_ERROR nullness/length, including denial/close/silence/env precedence and redirect controls. Static archive denial is null/0, synthetic ERRORproxy rows prove only a pure classifier. Production proxy classifier/veto decisions wait for measured supported signals; no opaque lws offset access or global-log inference. Cost if wrong: one extra small plan cycle, avoiding guessed credential/redirect policy.
- Ruling: preserve Consumed(Bytes) and GetPendingInboundBytes as payload-byte APIs, including batched/partial/zero acknowledgements. Add private queued-event slot pressure with initial high4096/low2048 and test overrides; pause when payload >M or slots >high, resume only when payload <=M/2 AND slots <=low and all other pause reasons allow. PopEvents appends at most its finite entry snapshot; dequeuing releases slot credit and can request a recheck without Consumed(0). Why: bytes cannot identify empty-message delivery; cost if wrong: a tunable private limit and finite-pop behavioral tightening, no signature change.
- Ruling: W3 admits at most one transport batch per connection across decode+delivery until consumed/discarded; notifications coalesce and gate release clears/rechecks pending work. Queue+one batch is the owned metadata bound; arbitrary public-consumer retained arrays and app-generated work are outside this inbound bound. Do not claim the logical M+message+64KiB payload allowance is an exact heap ceiling. Cost if wrong: less decode/delivery pipelining per connection; adjust only with an explicit finite window and proof.
- Ruling: SW-5 auto-replies remain outside application Send admission/buffer accounting, while ingress flow control may pause future reply-producing input. Add independent reply count/storage pause reasons, initially count high1024/low512, retained-storage highM/lowM/2 (private test overrides); charge retained storage including headroom and represent held empty replies explicitly. First exact matching rule wins; validate a configured reply against finite MaxMessageBytes before connecting. Preserve no-drop for accepted replies, message-boundary priority, existing close deadline, and ping/idle suspension until all pause reasons clear. Consumer credit never clears reply pressure; blocked writes must continue while reads pause. Cost if wrong: large autoreplies need a larger configured message limit and ingress may pause earlier; no unbounded warning-only lane or invented write-stall timeout.
- Implementation proof obligations: clear coalescing bit before service reads pressure; consumer credit posts recheck without relying on a stale paused flag; pause-side recheck closes consumer-before-paused race; terminal/shutdown commands capture IDs/weak service, never raw wsi. B4 measures packed empty/tiny frames and finite-pop refill, B6 measures independent reply pressure under blocked writes, W3 proves bounded admitted delivery. Keep VaRest-81s.5 open until its transport and W3 obligations are covered.

This is an ordinary incremental build of unchanged tracked Iris/plugin source after response/generated-file freshness, independent of the strict NoPCH gate. Recheck a fresh normal dry-run against the same action signatures and input hashes before execution; stop on expansion, PCH creation, unexpected edits or changed flags. Keep inputs stable; native UBT replans, so the prior graph is evidence, not an atomic allowlist. Then run the complete normal suite against the replaced libraries. Why: the next ordinary runner already needs this bounded closure; no engine source modification or cache/timestamp editing is needed. Cost if wrong: replace bounded normal artifacts and investigate build/runtime failure; do not proceed to broader engine rebuild. NoPCH remains separately guarded and unauthorized until recovery evidence is reviewed.

## M4b initial planning rulings (2026-10-23, Codex)
- Ruling: use an opaque fresh session cache identity on replacement/clear/expiry, preserve it on refresh, and carry it additively in ExportSession with a domain-separated length-framed SHA256 consistency binding of identity, tokens and canonical session headers. Validate complete import before mutation; a missing/mismatched binding imports otherwise valid session data with a fresh identity, while an unchanged valid export restores its identity. Identity mutation belongs in validated session/token mutation paths, not only OnSessionChanged (unchanged tokens can suppress that event). The exported session is caller-owned trusted credential state; this unkeyed hash detects mixed/stale fields, not malicious rewriting with a recomputed hash. No authentication claim. Why: preserve refresh/restart cache and queue continuity while separating ordinary account replacement/import mistakes. Cost if wrong: stale/mixed exports lose local cache/queue continuity; hostile application-owned session tampering remains outside this consistency guarantee.
- Ruling: every durable queued item carries an opaque auth-identity scope; replay re-applies current credentials only after scope equality. A mismatched FIFO item ends through OnQueuedResult with Auth and is not sent; the queue advances. Replacement/clear/expiry separates scopes; refresh retains scope; trusted exported-session import can restore it after restart. This tightens the old unconditional-current-auth reading of core4.4: queued account-A operations must not silently become account-B writes. Custom auth needs an explicit stable identity contract for durable queueing, otherwise refuse that queue configuration. Why: stored operations outlive login changes. Cost if wrong: old-account items fail rather than waiting indefinitely or replaying after a future login; callers use account-scoped queue names for separate retention.
- Ruling: shared queue ownership is process-local per canonical storage root/exact-name hash, with one FIFO replay owner; incompatible live origins/connectivity instances are a Config conflict, not silently first-wins configuration. Queueable resolved URLs must match the client base origin; validate the actual URL again on loading/replay, not a persisted claimed-origin field. Why: current credentials must not be applied to a changed/forged queue destination. Cost if wrong: cross-origin requests cannot opt into durable queueing; ordinary nonqueued HTTP behavior is unchanged. Complete callback ownership and config-conflict behavior must be specified and tested in the plan.

- Ruling: persistence uses a separate complete-payload credential guard, never log-redaction/excerpt equality. Snapshot all current raw session/auth/game registered secret values without the logging MinBytes filter; match bounded full payloads and supported wire encodings on background tasks, and reject recognized secret-key/userinfo URL/JSON/form input instead of mutating replay bytes. Credential/session headers are excluded, with no applied-auth persistence. Unknown undeclared secrets cannot be discovered reliably and remain caller responsibility; no claim that a log descriptor proves binary safe. Bound item and guard work explicitly and test short, encoded, tail and binary known secrets, cancellation and unchanged persisted bytes. Why: log redaction deliberately filters short values and captures excerpts/descriptors. Cost if wrong: legitimate payloads containing even short registered values are refused; the explicit queue opt-in accepts this conservative behavior.
- Ruling: IN-1 Started needs the cache decision, so separate capture at the existing Send/Enter boundary (original timestamp, initiator/script frame, as-built headers/body excerpt ownership under trace mask) from its single emission after cache/queue preflight. Emit before any VaRest-owned note, dispatch, excerpt launch or completion, including early refusal/cancel/outer-answer paths; in-flight late-attach snapshots may show Partial with undecided CacheLookup=None. Preserve zero-detail cost and existing no-cache timing where possible; async lookup must not recapture a later Blueprint stack or lose a promised excerpt. Why: a disk lookup result is unavailable at the old immediate emission point. Cost if wrong: the start event arrives later while retaining original start time; tests/goldens must pin ordering, cancellation and mid-lookup attachment.

- Ruling (supersedes the preceding delayed-Started ruling after inspection of TracePoints::Started and Outer-stage ordering): retain immediate Started at the existing boundary, including BP stack/StartUtc/as-built headers and request excerpt ownership. Add explicit bCacheLookupPending (default false) to Started; eligible unresolved cache lookups start with CacheLookup=None/pending=true, not a predicted successful disk hit. Snapshots report current lookup/pending state; add final CacheLookup and bFromCache to Completed. Preserve thirteen event kinds and use existing cache Note kinds for store/revalidation/stale actions. Amend IN-1/IN-5 and goldens explicitly in the plan/owning implementation task. Why: postponing Started would entangle arbitrary custom Outer holds/answers with async lookup, cancellation and excerpt announcement lifetime; an additive final-disposition contract is simpler and truthful. Cost if wrong: future Inspector consumers must read final cache disposition from Completed and use pending state during lookup; existing no-cache capture/order stays intact. No guessed index intent is presented as a completed disk hit.

- M4b refinement: bSameIdentity=true is already an explicit trusted caller assertion. Import with an existing nonempty live session and this flag preserves its current opaque identity (even if additive metadata is absent/mismatched); default import uses validated exported identity or a fresh one on missing/mismatch. Without a live session, valid binding can restore identity for restart; otherwise mint fresh. Document precedence and test valid/old/mismatch/changed-header rows with both flags and no-session import. Why: compatibility with the existing same-account token-update contract; cost if wrong: a caller making a false same-identity assertion intentionally shares local scope, as the flag's trust contract states.
- M4b cache/queue limits: no-cache/must-revalidate entries cannot be served stale without validation. bServeStaleOnError covers Connection/Offline only, not Auth/HTTP5xx/Timeout. Approve finite default queue limits32MiB/item and64MiB aggregate in addition to256items/24h; specify whether budgets charge encoded on-disk bytes and include metadata/temp-write occupancy rather than claiming payload size bounds total storage. Why: prevent unbounded offline storage and prohibit unauthorized/stale validation bypass; cost if wrong: large payloads are refused and timeout/HTTPerrors do not use stale data.

- M4b queue admission ruling: accept two-phase persistence: worker writes/flushed nonreplayable .pending; GT rechecks owner/auth scope/cancel/deadline and selects final commit vs abort; final atomic rename and acknowledgement remain off-GT. Define the explicit acceptance linearization at GT commit handoff, with a shared atomic admission gate so any-thread Cancel races that same transition (a stale read of bCancelRequested plus a later plain store is insufficient). Cancel/deadline/owner before the gate wins leave no replayable item; after commit selection cancellation cannot promise rollback and OnQueued is delivered only after successful rename. Rename failure reports the actual failure, never a false Queued. State/IsActive/IsDone/docs must make this cutoff truthful, extend the existing documented terminal-IO exception without changing ordinary requests, and suppress callbacks for owner loss/shutdown while still draining committed IO. Pending files are ignored/cleaned on restart; charge reservations/temp files and drain all work. Why: durable acknowledgement must follow atomic persistence, and a canceled acknowledged request must not later reappear through a crash race. Cost if wrong: cancellation/deadline after commit selection cannot interrupt the filesystem operation; this exception needs deterministic before/after/racing worker+GT tests and independent lifecycle review.

- M4b connectivity ruling: update reachability only from the current actual transport dispatch in OnAttemptDone, never orphan/stale dispatches, cache/stale hits, custom synthetic outcomes or logical/envelope success. Connection failure sets offline; any real HTTP response absent Connection failure sets online, including HTTP401/404/503. The first ordinary network failure keeps Kind=Connection and its existing inner Retry cycle; a later nonqueued request entering known-offline gets cache/stale lookup, then Offline without wire on miss. An opted-in request entering offline queues immediately per core4.4; do not reorder the declared offline→cache seam. ForceOffline masks underlying state and suppresses probes, so delayed responses cannot clear the mask. Each FTestRig owns isolated connectivity; multiple independent negative scenarios explicitly restore online where appropriate. Why: reachability is a transport observation and cache hits cannot establish a network. Cost if wrong: callers see Connection on the discovering request and Offline on later known-offline misses; tests must pin both and existing M4a retry behavior.

- M4b storage portability ruling: cache/queue directories are app-owned state, not an adversarial local-filesystem sandbox. Use strict generated leaf names/no traversal, refuse known symlinks and test Linux symlink skipping; IPlatformFile::IsSymlink may return Unimplemented, so do not claim universal containment against malicious concurrent local replacement without platform nofollow/openat support. Flush(true)+atomic rename establishes complete-file visibility and tested process-crash recovery; generic IPlatformFile provides no directory-fsync guarantee, so do not claim power-loss durability. Why: the portable API cannot prove stronger properties. Cost if wrong: unsupported-platform concurrent local tampering and sudden power loss can corrupt/lose state; malformed/lost entries must be handled safely, and native validation remains M7.

- ForceOffline refinement: retain observed connectivity separately from effective published connectivity (observed && !forced). Clearing force reveals the latest underlying observation and emits an effective transition with reason OverrideRemoved; schedule probes only when the underlying state is offline. Physical in-flight outcomes can update observation while forced but cannot clear the effective forced-offline mask. Do not invent a mandatory fresh probe on override removal. Why: this follows the already-ruled separate simulation override; cost if wrong: removing simulation can immediately reveal the last observed online state, whose age remains an observation rather than a fresh connectivity guarantee.

- M4b persistence-guard work bound accepted: KMP per needle, at most64 unique wire-form needles,16KiB each/64KiB aggregate,128Mi comparison steps per complete candidate scan, cancellation checked at least every4096 steps through worker-safe atomic abort state. Cost if wrong: many/large registered secrets or expensive payloads conservatively skip cache persistence or fail durable queue admission rather than retaining secrets or monopolizing a worker.

- M4b shared disk-store capacity ruling: use the minimum capacity ever requested during that store instance's lifetime; it may decrease but does not increase until all holders/workers release it and a new instance opens. State this contract explicitly instead of claiming live-minimum lease recovery. Apply capacity/eviction changes through the serial worker boundary; document any transient in-flight allowance, and test a smaller registrant followed by release/reopen. Why: caching capacity is an upper bound, and conservative retention avoids additional lease/shutdown edges. Cost if wrong: a surviving holder can keep a smaller-than-current-policy cache until the shared instance is released; this changes hit rate, not cross-user isolation or request correctness.

- M4b shutdown planning refinement: persistence completion must drain only executor-owned results/continuations, never ProcessThreadUntilIdle on the global game-thread queue during Stop. Why: a global GT pump can run unrelated callbacks and reenter shutdown; cost if wrong: one explicit bounded handback queue and ticker lifecycle to review, rather than treating a global task drain as isolated.

- M4b CacheStored ruling: emit a request CacheStored Note only when synchronous memory admission actually retains the key, before Completed; Bytes describes retained encoded response bytes, not durable disk bytes. Best-effort asynchronous disk persistence never emits a late request Note, predicts success, or delays successful response delivery. A response persisted only to disk may have no CacheStored Note. Amend Inspector IN-4/IN-17 and docs in the owning implementation task; a delayed/failing disk-write golden must prove prompt successful completion and no late Note. Why: traces close at Completed and admitted IO is not successful storage; cost if wrong: the request trace omits later disk-store outcomes, which require a separately designed store-level diagnostic rather than reopening records or adding an event kind now.

- M4b FlushQueue admission ruling: code-first bool FlushQueue(callback = {}) reports whether an asynchronous flush request was accepted. False means no callback for invalid client/registration, shutdown, or bounded waiter/owned-continuation refusal; true completes asynchronously with the IO result, subject to explicit shutdown/owner callback suppression. Never report overflow through an inline callback or retain a waiter before acquiring its bounded continuation slot. Pin non-inline success, accepted IO failure, overflow/no-callback and shutdown tests; carry boolean refusal handling into M5 teardown/BP wrappers. Why: finite retained callbacks cannot promise delivery for unlimited rejected submissions, and this API is not shipped yet. Cost if wrong: callers must handle immediate admission refusal separately from an accepted flush's later storage outcome.
